● Findings before coding:
  - Spring Boot 4.1.1 is the latest 4.1.x. Gradle 9.8.2 is current.
    eclipse-temurin:25-jdk and 25-jre exist.
  - picocli-spring-boot-starter 4.7.7 registers itself through
    AutoConfiguration.imports, so Boot 4 should pick it up. It pulls in Boot 2.7
    transitively, but the Boot BOM will override that. I'll confirm by running
    it.
  - data/adp_vintages.csv doesn't exist yet. Per P6 it gets committed later, so
    for now make init can only build the image and create the volume.

  Open questions

  1. Is Spring Boot over-engineering for a CLI of ~15 objects?

  The strongest case for manual wiring:
  - The whole object graph is about 15 constructors. A single Main composition
    root would be around 40 lines of plain new, readable top to bottom, with no
    reflection and no proxies.
  - Mistakes would show up as compile errors instead of startup exceptions.
  - Startup would be roughly 100 ms instead of 1–2 s on every CLI call. That
    matters for a tool you run interactively.
  - The fat jar would be much smaller, and picocli could work without a factory
    bridge.
  - The design keeps the core free of Spring anyway, so the container only ever
    wires about 8 edge objects. Spring is paying for flexibility we mostly don't
    use.

  What we keep by using Spring anyway:
  - Plug-in registration by bean collection. Injecting Map<String, Forecaster>
    and Map<String, HistoryWindow> means the D-extension demo
    (MovingAverageForecaster, Rolling) really is one class plus one @Bean, with
    no hand-edited registry.
  - Externalised configuration. spring.datasource.url, profiles and env-var
    overrides come for free, so switching to Postgres is a property change, as
    D9 says.
  - Conditional adapters. @ConditionalOnProperty gives us the S3 repository
    path, and FredSource only when a key is present.
  - Free auto-configuration. DataSource/JdbcClient and the test slice
    (@SpringBootTest) need no setup.
  - Reversible choice. Because Spring lives only in config/, adapter/ and cli/,
    replacing it means rewriting config/ and nothing else.

  The trade-off is about 1–2 s of startup and some indirection in exchange for
  extensibility you can show with a one-@Bean diff.

  2. Spotless or Checkstyle?

  Spotless (google-java-format). For a solo take-home, the useful lint is "is
  the code consistently formatted". Spotless enforces that with zero rule tuning
  and can fix it itself (spotlessApply), so lint failures cost seconds.

  Checkstyle reports problems but can't fix them. It also needs a rule file that
  I would have to curate and defend, and its value is enforcing team
  conventions across many authors, which doesn't apply here. The rule that
  actually matters in this project, "no Spring in the core", is architectural,
  and neither tool checks it. That would be ArchUnit, which isn't in the pattern
  map, so I'm listing it as a follow-up rather than adding it.

● P0 is done: all four acceptance checks pass, starting from a clean image
  build.

  - make init: builds the image and creates the database volume.
  - make run ARGS="--help": lists history, forecast, backtest and sync.
  - make test: passes; Gradle reports the empty suite as NO-SOURCE.
  - make lint: passes.

  I answered the two open questions (Spring vs. manual wiring, and Spotless)
  before coding; they're above, ready to copy into PROMPTS.md. I chose Spotless
  with google-java-format because it fixes formatting itself and needs no rule
  file. The rule that matters here, keeping Spring out of the core, is
  architectural, and neither Spotless nor Checkstyle checks it.

  What was built
  - Build: Spring Boot 4.1.1, Java 25, Gradle 9.8.2 wrapper (Kotlin DSL), with
    exactly the dependencies P0 lists. For tests I used
    spring-boot-starter-test, which includes JUnit 5 and AssertJ and is needed
    for the @SpringBootTest in P5.
  - Packages: the seven from D7 under adp.*, each with a package-info.java
    describing its layer. The core packages contain no Spring imports.
  - Wiring: AdpApplication only scans adp.config, so concrete classes can only
    come from @Bean methods there. Picocli builds the commands through Spring,
    so P5's commands can take Map<String, Forecaster> in their constructor
    without being annotated.
  - Runtime settings: no web server, no banner, logging at WARN, and SQLite
    stored at /data/adp.db on the named volume.
  - Docker and Make: a two-stage Dockerfile; the build stage is reused for test,
    lint and an extra format target that fixes formatting in your local files.

  Things that differ from the plan
  1. Formatter version: google-java-format is pinned to 1.36.1. The latest,
     1.37.0, crashes inside Spotless 8.10.4, which itself bundles 1.36.1.
     There's a comment in build.gradle.kts.
  2. make init doesn't seed the database yet: data/adp_vintages.csv doesn't
     exist until P6, and nothing can read it until P2. For now it builds the
     image and creates the volume.
  3. make refresh-snapshot is a placeholder: it checks that FRED_API_KEY is set,
     then exits with "not implemented yet". I didn't want to invent sync
     options before P2/P5 define them.
  4. Unimplemented subcommands fail on purpose: they print "not implemented yet"
     and exit 1 rather than succeeding silently.

  Follow-ups (not built)
  - Wire seeding into make init and the FRED pull into refresh-snapshot (P2/P5).
  - ArchUnit could enforce "no Spring in the core" in tests, but it isn't in the
    pattern map, so it needs your OK first.

  Nothing is committed yet. You're on main, and D11's one commit per phase is
  yours to make once you've added this session to PROMPTS.md.

● I've read P1. CLAUDE.md and DESIGN.md are unchanged since P0, so my reading of
  D1 and D9 still holds. These are my answers to P1's open questions, plus one
  problem I found in D1's wording. Nothing will be coded until you decide.

  Q1 — Should PrintHistory be a rich class or a record plus a service?

  A rich, immutable final class PrintHistory.

  - asOf is easiest to test this way. asOf only needs the data and a date. As a
    method on the class, a test is three lines: build a history from a few
    observations, call history.asOf(date), and assert on the PrintHistory it
    returns. A separate service would add an object to construct and a seam with
    only one implementation. It would also leave PrintHistory as a bag of data,
    against the "domain owns behavior" rule.
  - Class rather than record. A record can only store its components.
    PrintHistory should build its month → vintages index once, in the
    constructor, and keep it private. A record would expose the raw list through
    its accessor and leave the index to be rebuilt on every call. The things
    that do suit records (ReferenceMonth, JobsChange, Observation) stay records.

  Q2 — Do we need Print, or is PrintHistory over raw observations enough?

  The smaller model holds up: Observation (one D9 row) plus PrintHistory, with 
  no Print.

  The deciding reason is that Print can't own its most important behavior. A
  first print or a revision of the change for month M needs M−1's level from the
  same vintage. That is a calculation across two months. A Print that only
  knows M can do nothing except hold a list of levels; the logic still has to
  live in PrintHistory. So it would be an entity in name only, and the extra
  type would make you ask "where does this logic go?" every time.

  What we would lose is a type for one month's revision trail, which P5's
  history command might want. PrintHistory can answer that with
  vintagesOf(month), which needs no new type.

  This contradicts a settled decision. D1 and the CLAUDE.md pattern map both
  name Print as the Entity. Per CLAUDE.md, I'm raising this rather than working
  around it. Your options:
  - (a) Drop Print. I'd update D1 and the pattern map: the Entity row would go,
    and Observation would join the value-object row.
  - (b) Keep Print as a thin read model. It would be a record Print(month, 
    List<Observation> trail) that PrintHistory derives on request and never
    stores. That keeps the docs true for little cost, but it's mostly ceremony.

  I recommend (a).

  Two things D1 doesn't spell out

  1. "Same vintage" has to mean the level as of the vintage date, not a row with
  exactly that date. ALFRED reports a value once, with the date it became valid
  (realtime_start). It does not repeat unchanged months in every later vintage.
  So the vintage that first published M (say 2026-10-01) usually has no row for
  M−1 dated exactly 2026-10-01, because M−1 wasn't revised that day.

  The correct rule is: first-print change(M) = asOf(V).level(M) − 
  asOf(V).level(M−1), where V is M's earliest vintage date. This also works if
  the data does repeat every month in every vintage, so it is safe under either
  storage style. Under an exact-match reading, the code would throw or skip
  months on real data. If M−1 has no level at V (M is the first month in the
  series), there is no first-print change, and I'd return Optional.empty().

  2. "Null levels are rejected" and the type of the level. A primitive long 
  level makes a null level impossible to construct, which is a stronger
  guarantee than a runtime check. But a test can't pass null to a primitive, so
  your stop criterion couldn't be tested literally. I'd use long and test the 
  rejection of negative or zero levels, with null rejection tested on month and
  vintageDate. If you want the literal test, I'll use Long with requireNonNull.

  The plan once you decide

  - Domain types (tests first):
    - ReferenceMonth(YearMonth) with previous()/next().
    - JobsChange(long), the change in persons, rendered as thousands later.
    - Observation(ReferenceMonth, LocalDate vintageDate, long level), which
      rejects a vintage dated before the end of its reference month.
    - PrintHistory with asOf, latest(), firstPrintChange, changes() and
      vintagesOf.
  - ArchUnit: I'll add archunit-junit5, checking first that the release I pick
    reads Java 25 class files. An ArchitectureTest will fail the build if
    domain, forecast, evaluation or port depend on org.springframework... The
    new pattern-map row:
    | Architecture rules | Fitness function (test) | ArchitectureTest (ArchUnit)
    | Checkstyle import rules |

  I need two decisions: for Q2, (a) or (b); and the level type, long or Long.

● P1 is done. make test and make lint both pass, with 26 tests across the domain
  classes and the new architecture test. Nothing is committed yet.

  Your stop criteria
  - First-print change uses the vintage that first published M: passes.
    PrintHistory.firstPrintChange reads both levels as of that vintage date V,
    and two tests cover your cases:
    - M−1 was revised exactly at V. The revised value is used, and a later
      revision of M is ignored.
    - M−1 has no row at V. Its earlier value is carried forward, and a revision
      of M−1 published after V is ignored.
    - It returns empty when M or M−1 is unknown.
  - asOf(date) takes the latest vintage on or before date and excludes later 
    ones: passes. The tests check:
    - the latest vintage on or before date for every month, with a vintage dated
      exactly on date included;
    - later vintages and months first published after date are dropped,
      including from firstPrintChange;
    - a date before any release gives an empty history, and the original is
      unchanged.
  - Invalid input is rejected: passes. That covers month 0 or 13, unparseable
    text, null fields, and zero or negative levels (in place of the null-level
    criterion, as you decided). I also added one check: a vintage dated before
    its month has ended is rejected, because that can't be a real publication.
  - ArchUnit test: passes. I also confirmed it fails the build when I plant a
    Spring import in domain/; that test ran in a throwaway container, so nothing
    was left in your tree.

  What's in the domain now
  - ReferenceMonth wraps YearMonth, with of, parse, previous, next and
    endOfMonth.
  - JobsChange holds the change in persons.
  - Observation is one stored row: month, vintage date and level.
  - PrintHistory is the immutable final class you agreed, with asOf, latest(),
    changes(), firstPrintChange and vintagesOf.
  - latest() returns the series as it stood at the newest vintage held. So
    changes() never mixes vintages either.
  - If the same month and vintage arrive twice with different levels,
    PrintHistory rejects them. Exact duplicates are accepted.

  Doc updates
  - D1: Print is gone and Observation added. The same-vintage rule now reads "as
    of V", with the carry-forward explained. The note about primitive long and
    non-positive levels is added, along with a "Cut (P1)" note on why there is
    no Print.
  - D7: the domain package list no longer mentions Print.
  - CLAUDE.md pattern map: the Domain row now lists value objects only, with
    vintagesOf() for revision trails and Print entity under "deliberately not
    used". There is also the new Architecture rules row for ArchitectureTest.
  - I left the P1 text in docs/PROMPTS-PLAN.md unchanged as the record of the
    prompt you sent, even though it still mentions Print and null levels.

  Build and test changes: ArchUnit 1.5.1 is added as a test dependency, and the
  tests are new under src/test/.

  Should I commit this as the P1 commit, the same way as P0?

● I've read P2. Here are my answers, sourced where they're factual. There's also
  a bug in P1's validation that this research turned up, and one blocker for
  P2's acceptance criteria.

  Q1 — How far back do ALFRED vintages go, and what did the 2022 relaunch do?

  Coverage starts on 2022-08-31, the relaunch day. ALFRED's page for
  ADPMNUSNERSA lists its first vintage on that date. That is the day ADP
  reintroduced the National Employment Report after pausing it in mid-2022. ADP
  rebuilt the methodology with the Stanford Digital Economy Lab, turning the
  report from a forecast of the BLS number into an independent measure of
  employment.

  What the relaunch means for the data:
  - Before 2022-08-31, this series didn't exist in real time. The 2010 to Jul
    2022 levels in the first vintage are a backcast under the new method. ADP's
    pre-2022 report was a different product (a BLS forecast), so splicing its
    vintages on would mix two concepts. I'd reject that.
  - Every February, ADP rewrites the whole history. The annual revision
    reweights the series to QCEW benchmarks back to 2010. The February 2026 one
    moved the level down by about 2.5M across the series and changed many
    monthly changes.
  - Our first-print rule already handles the February rewrites. A January's
    first print reads the benchmarked December from that same vintage, asOf(V),
    so no vintages are mixed.

  What that leaves for the backtest:
  - About 49 real first-print targets. Those run from Sep 2022 to Sep 2026, each
    forecast from the vintage before that month's release. Months up to Aug
    2022 have no real-time first print, since their first appearance is the
    relaunch backcast. I'm estimating the count from the release dates; I can't
    count the vintages exactly without an API key (see the blocker below).
  - Look-ahead stays clean for these. For any forecast origin after 2022-08-31,
    asOf gives exactly what a forecaster had then, including the backcast
    history.
  - The test window contains no COVID months. In the real-time backtest,
    exclude-covid can only change the fit, not the scoring. That affects how
    D6's comparison table is read in P4 and P6.

  The honest fallback. D2 already says to fall back to a latest-vintage backtest
  if coverage is short. I'd refine that:
  - The headline is the real-time backtest: about 49 first prints, with no
    look-ahead. The sample is small, so the reported MAE carries roughly ±15%
    sampling noise, and the README should say so.
  - A secondary backtest uses the latest vintage over 2011 to 2026: about 190
    targets, labelled biased. Both the fit and the "truth" use revised,
    benchmarked data, and the truth is the current estimate rather than the
    first print. It's useful for the window sensitivity check, including the
    COVID scoring question, but it is not the headline number.
  - I'd update D2's "To verify during build" note with these findings. This
    mainly matters for P4 (Backtester) and P6 (README), but it is a change to
    D2, so it's your decision.

  API details, checked against FRED's docs. Call fred/series/observations with:
  - realtime_start=1776-07-04 and realtime_end=9999-12-31, set explicitly. Both
    default to today, which would silently return only the current vintage.
  - output_type=1 (observations by real-time period) and file_type=json.

  Each row has date, value (a string) and realtime_start. realtime_start maps to
  our vintage_date. Rows only appear when a value changes, which is exactly the
  carry-forward storage that asOf(V) assumes.

  Q2 — Should sync live in the CLI or behind an application service?

  In the CLI. SyncCommand should call the two ports directly. The whole flow is
  about ten lines:
  1. Pick the source from Map<String, PrintSource>.
  2. Fetch the observations.
  3. Call repository.saveAll(...), which returns the number of new rows.
  4. Optionally export to a file.
  5. Print the count.

  It's the easier option to test. Picocli commands are plain classes with
  constructor injection. A test builds new SyncCommand(Map.of("csv", 
  fakeSource), inMemoryRepo), runs it through CommandLine.execute, and checks
  the output. It needs no Spring context.

  A service would add an application/ layer that isn't in D7 or the pattern map.
  CLAUDE.md asks me to argue before adding one, and with a single caller I
  can't make that case. If make init ever needs multi-source orchestration (CSV,
  then FRED when a key is set), I'd extract a service then.

  The CSV format (header, column order) should be defined once, in adapter/,
  shared by CsvSource and --export. That keeps file I/O out of the CLI.

  A bug in P1 that the research turned up

  Observation rejects any vintage dated on or before the last day of its month.
  Real releases break that:
  - The relaunch: ADP published August 2022 on 2022-08-31, the last day of the
    month, and that is the first vintage in the series.
  - Releases two days early: I believe August 2023 was released on 2023-08-30.
    ADP publishes on the Wednesday before the BLS Friday, which can fall inside
    the reference month.

  As written, P1 would reject the very first row of real data. My fix would
  relax the rule to "the vintage can't precede the first day of the reference
  month". That still catches swapped columns. I'd add regression tests for both
  dates. This changes P1 code, so I'd fold it into P2 unless you'd rather have a
  separate commit.

  Blocker — there's no data for sync --source csv

  FRED_API_KEY isn't set here, and data/adp_vintages.csv isn't due until P6.
  Without vintage data, P2's acceptance step ("reports the number of new
  vintages; twice reports 0") has nothing to load. FRED's public fredgraph.csv
  only serves the latest vintage; I tested that it ignores vintage_date.
  Options:
  - (a) Provide a key (recommended). Free keys are at
    fred.stlouisfed.org/docs/api/api_key.html. With it I can run FredSource
    against the live API, confirm the exact vintage count for Q1, and generate
    the real data/adp_vintages.csv now. That brings P6's snapshot step forward.
  - (b) Use a small hand-made sample CSV now. It would be labelled as sample
    data, and the real snapshot would wait for P6. Acceptance would pass, but
    against fake numbers.

  Decisions I need:
  1. Q1: real-time headline plus a disclosed latest-vintage secondary, and
     update D2 now?
  2. Q2: sync in the CLI?
  3. Fix the Observation date rule within P2?
  4. Blocker: (a) or (b)?

  Sources:

    (https://alfred.stlouisfed.org/series?seid=ADPMNUSNERSA)
  - FRED API: series/observations
    (https://fred.stlouisfed.org/docs/api/fred/series_observations.html)
  - ADP: New National Employment Report to launch late summer 2022
    (https://mediacenter.adp.com/2022-06-30-New-ADP-National-Employment-Report-t
    o-Launch-Late-Summer-2022)
  - Stanford Digital Economy Lab: National Employment Report
    (https://digitaleconomy.stanford.edu/project/national-employment-report-adp)
  - Fox Business: ADP revamping national employment report (https://foxbusiness.
    com/economy/adp-revamping-national-employment-report-methodology)
  - Marketplace: what goes into calculating employment numbers (https://marketpl
    ace.org/2022/08/31/what-goes-into-calculating-employment-numbers)
  - Wolf Street: ADP report revised back to 2010 (Feb 2026) (https://wolfstreet.
    com/2026/02/04/adp-employment-report-massively-revised-back-to-2010-with-hug
    e-erratic-differences-in-month-to-month-job-creation-losses/)

      35 +          continue;                                            
      36 +        }                                                      
      37 +        if (!headerSeen) {                                     
      38 +          if (!trimmed.equals(HEADER)) {                       
      39 +            throw new IllegalArgumentException(                
      40 +                "line " + lineNumber + ": expected header '" + 
         +HEADER + "'");

● P2 is implemented and P2's acceptance criteria pass. make test passes with 50
  tests, all confirmed to have run, and make lint passes. The main P2 work isn't
  committed yet. Only the Observation fix is: it's its own commit, 0e8eadf, as
  you asked.

  Acceptance
  - JdbcPrintRepository: round-trips observations on a temp SQLite file using
    the real schema.sql. Re-inserting returns 0, a stored vintage is never
    overwritten, and a mixed batch counts only the new rows.
  - CsvSource: parses a fixture, and reads back exactly what --export wrote.
  - FredSource: parses a FRED-format JSON fixture served by a local JDK HTTP
    server, so no network is used. The tests also check:
    - the exact query parameters sent to FRED;
    - that rows with value "." are skipped;
    - that it refuses to run without a key;
    - that HTTP errors don't leak the key.
  - sync, run twice on a fresh volume: sync: 12 read from csv, 12 new, then
    sync: 12 read from csv, 0 new.

  Your decisions, as implemented
  - D2: "To verify during build" is replaced by the verified findings. That
    covers the 2022-08-31 relaunch start, the February rewrites, the API
    parameters, the real-time headline backtest with the ±15% noise note, and
    the biased latest-vintage secondary. The ~49 target count is marked as an
    estimate.
  - sync: SyncCommand calls the ports directly, with no application/ layer. Its
    tests run through CommandLine.execute with fakes and no Spring context.
  - Observation fix: in 0e8eadf, with regression tests for 2022-08-31 and
    2023-08-30.
  - Sample data: data/sample_adp_vintages.csv has "SAMPLE DATA… Not published by
    ADP or FRED" in its header, and the real data/adp_vintages.csv is left for
    P6. The default CSV source points at the sample for now.

  Things I added that you should know about
  - New port: SnapshotWriter → CsvSnapshotWriter. It lets --export write CSV
    without the CLI depending on a concrete adapter class. It's the same Adapter
    pattern as PrintSource, and both share one package-private CsvFormat. I
    added it to D7 and the pattern-map row. If you'd rather not have the extra
    interface, the alternative is for the CLI to call the adapter directly.
  - New dependency: Jackson 3 (jackson-databind, version managed by Boot). It
    parses FRED's JSON and is only used in adapter/.
  - The FRED fixture isn't a real recording. It's written to FRED's documented
    response format. There's no key here, so FredSource hasn't parsed a real
    response yet. A dummy-key run did reach the live API and failed cleanly with
    HTTP 400.
  - One-line errors for every command. CliRunner now prints any command failure
    as error: … instead of a stack trace.
  - make refresh-snapshot is wired up. It runs sync --source fred --export 
    data/adp_vintages.csv as your host user, so the file isn't root-owned. It
    needs a key to produce data.
  - JDK 25 warning on every run. sqlite-jdbc's native load printed a "restricted
    method" warning each time. An Enable-Native-Access attribute in the jar
    manifest silences it.
  - --export writes what the source returned, not the whole database. That keeps
    the snapshot equal to exactly what FRED published.

  Left for later
  - make init seeding: it still doesn't seed the database. Doing that now would
    load sample numbers into the default database, so it belongs with P6's real
    snapshot.
  - Database on every start: the app now runs schema.sql at startup, so even
    --help opens the database. That's harmless inside Docker, but outside Docker
    it needs ADP_DATA_DIR to point at a writable directory.

  Should I commit the rest as the P2 commit?

❯ Read P3 in PROMPTS-PLAN.md and answer its open questions first                

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● I've read P3, and D4–D6 are as I last saw them. Here are my answers to its two
  open questions, plus two issues P3 doesn't ask about but needs settled before
  coding.

  Q1 — With only two models, is a Forecaster interface over-engineering?

  The strongest case against it. The naive forecaster is just AR(1) with the
  intercept fixed at 0 and the coefficient at 1, and no fitting. So the honest
  minimal model is a single AutoregressiveForecaster with a fixed mode. That
  would mean one class, no interface, and no Strategy, with the CLI choosing
  between two configurations of the same class. Any third model that matters (a
  moving average) is also a linear combination of lags, so the abstraction would
  only ever be guarding one shape. YAGNI says to add the interface when a
  non-linear model shows up.

  Decision: keep the interface. Three concrete uses it serves now:
  1. P4's look-ahead guard needs a fake model. That test passes a spy Forecaster
     that records which PrintHistory it received and asserts nothing was
     published after the as-of date. Without an interface, the guard would have
     to inspect a real model.
  2. The backtester runs "the model" and "the baseline" through the same code 
     path. Skill vs. naive is 1 − MAE_model / MAE_naive, computed identically
     for both. That is polymorphism, whatever the classes are called.
  3. Map<String, Forecaster> is the extension point D3 chose Spring for. P7's
     "one class + one @Bean" claim depends on it.

  The cost is a two-method interface. Folding naive into AR would also blur the
  explanation: "last print" is a clearer story than "1.00 × Sep".

  Q2 — Should Explanation be model-specific (sealed per model) or a generic 
  list?

  Neither extreme. I recommend a generic list of terms with a small sealed set 
  of term shapes.

  - Sealed per model (ArExplanation, NaiveExplanation, …) is type-safe, but the
    CLI renderer would have to pattern-match on every model. P7's
    MovingAverageForecaster would then need a renderer edit, which breaks the
    extension demo's "no edits elsewhere".
  - A flat List<(String label, amount)> keeps the renderer model-agnostic. But
    the model would pre-format labels like "0.45 × Sep (+143K)", which puts
    rendering and units (K) into forecast/.
  - Recommended:
  record Explanation(List<Term> terms)
  sealed interface Term permits Constant, Weighted
  record Constant(String name, JobsChange amount)                        // 
  intercept
  record Weighted(double weight, ReferenceMonth month, JobsChange input) // 
  amount = round(weight × input)
    The sealed set covers term shapes, not models:
    - AR = one Constant + p Weighted terms.
    - Naive = Weighted(1.0, Sep, +143K).
    - A moving average would be n Weighted(1/n, …) terms.

    The renderer handles two shapes once and never changes for a new model.

  Exact sums by construction. JobsChange is whole jobs (long), but OLS gives a
  double. If each term's amount is rounded to whole jobs and the point is
  defined as the sum of the term amounts, the sum is exact by definition.
  Forecast's compact constructor would then enforce point == Σ amounts, so a
  forecast that breaks the invariant can't be constructed. Compared with
  rounding the raw OLS value, this differs by at most p+1 jobs, which is
  invisible at the K scale.

  One issue for P5: each row in the D8 output is rounded to thousands
  independently, so the displayed rows may not visibly add up (42+64+11+1 could
  show 117 against 118). It is a display problem, not a model problem, and I'm
  just flagging it now.

  Two issues P3 doesn't ask about

  1. Excluding COVID and AR lags.

  D6's window works on PrintHistory, so Excluding removes levels for Mar 2020 –
  Dec 2021, matching your acceptance wording. Two consequences:
  - We lose one extra change, Jan 2022. changes() needs M−1's level, so the
    change series loses Mar 2020 – Jan 2022. That's one ordinary month out of
    about 190, and I'd document it.
  - The AR must not bridge the gap. Done naively, the regression would treat Feb
    2020 and Feb 2022 as consecutive and pair them as target and lag. I'd only
    build a regression row when the target month and all p lag months exist in
    the series. That also drops Feb–Apr 2022 as targets, which is correct.

  The alternative is to filter the change series instead of levels. That would
  keep Jan 2022, but it changes D6's HistoryWindow signature and what
  forecasters receive. I don't think one month is worth that. I recommend 
  keeping D6 as written and documenting the one lost month.

  2. What the AR fits on. It fits on asOf.changes(): the series as it stood at
  the as-of date, revised where revisions had been published. That is what a
  forecaster had at the time. The newest month's value is its first print,
  because it hasn't been revised yet. So the explanation's "Sep (+143K)" is the
  number the market saw, and naive's "last print" is that same value.

  Other details, unless you object

  - MonthRange becomes a domain value object (from ≤ to, inclusive, validated).
    Excluding lives in forecast/.
  - AllHistory is the identity window. andThen(next) applies this, then next.
  - When there's too little data, the AR fails with a clear message naming how
    many complete rows it needs versus how many it has. It needs more rows than
    its p+1 parameters, which is OLS's own requirement.
  - p comes from adp.ar.order (default 3) and is passed to the constructor in
    config/. That keeps forecast/ free of Spring.
  - The AR coefficient test generates a seeded synthetic AR(3) series of about
    2,000 points. It checks the recovered coefficients to within ±0.05 and the
    intercept to within a few percent.

  Decisions I need:
  1. Q1: keep the interface?
  2. Q2: the term-shape Explanation, with point = Σ term amounts enforced in
     Forecast?
  3. The COVID window: keep D6's level-based window, document the lost Jan 2022
     change, and require consecutive lags?

      10 +final class Histories {                                        
      11 +

      13 +                                                               
      14 +  private Histories() {}                                       
      15 +                                                               
      16 +  /** Level for {@code first.previous()} is the base; each chan
         +ge adds one month after it. */                                 
      17 +  static PrintHistory fromChanges(ReferenceMonth first, List<Lo
         +ng> changes) {                                                 
      18 +    var observations = new ArrayList<Observation>();           
      19 +    var month = first.previous();                              
      20 +    long level = BASE_LEVEL;                                   
      21 +    observations.add(new Observation(month, month.next().firstD
         +ay(), level));                                                 
      22 +    for (long change : changes) {                              
      23 +      month = month.next();                                    
      24 +      level += change;                                         
      25 +      observations.add(new Observation(month, month.next().firs
         +tDay(), level));                                               
      26 +    }                                                          
      27 +    return new PrintHistory(observations);                     
      28 +  }                                                            
      29 +                                                               
      30 +  static PrintHistory fromChanges(ReferenceMonth first, long...
         + changes) {                                                    
      31 +    var list = new ArrayList<Long>();                          
      32 +    for (long c : changes) {                                   
      33 +      list.add(c);                                             
      34 +    }                                                          
      35 +    return fromChanges(first, list);                           
      36 +  }                                                            
      37 +}

● Update(docs/DESIGN.md)
  ⎿  Added 10 lines
       99  
      100  **Why only two:** build exactly what was asked; prove extensib
           ility with a final small commit instead.
      101  
      102 +**Explanation (P3):** a list of terms in two shapes, not one t
          +ype per model: `Constant(name, amount)` (the                  
      103 +intercept) and `Weighted(weight, month, input)` (amount = `rou
          +nd(weight × input)` in whole jobs). Naive is one              
      104 +`Weighted(1.0, …)`; AR is the intercept plus p `Weighted` lags
          +. The renderer handles two shapes once, so a new              
      105 +linear model needs no CLI change. The point forecast is define
          +d as the sum of the term amounts, and                         
      106 +`Forecast` rejects any point that differs, so contributions su
          +m exactly by construction.                                    
      107 +                                                              
      108 +**Missing months (P3):** a model that lacks the months it need
          +s throws `InsufficientHistoryException` (e.g. AR              
      109 +needs changes for all p months before the target). D4's signat
          +ure is unchanged; callers decide whether it is                
      110 +an error (CLI) or a skipped month (backtest).                 
      111 +                                                              
      112  **Cut:** moving average, ensemble, prediction intervals (an in
           terval would also need its coverage evaluated).
      113  
      114  ### D5 — Accuracy: walk-forward backtest against first prints

● Update(docs/DESIGN.md)
  ⎿  Added 3 lines
      120  - **Skill vs. naive** = `1 − MAE_model / MAE_naive` — positive
            means the model beats the baseline.
      121  - `AccuracyMetric` is a strategy; only these two ship.
      122  - AR order `p` is fixed up front (not tuned on the backtest), 
           so the reported score stays honest.
      123 +- **Compare like with like (P4):** score only months where eve

● P3 is implemented. make test and make lint both pass, with 77 tests in total.
  Nothing is committed yet.

  Your stop criteria
  - AR recovers known coefficients: passes. On a seeded synthetic AR(3) series
    of 2,000 points it estimates φ = 0.543 / 0.203 / 0.065 against true values
    of 0.5 / 0.2 / 0.1, all within ±0.05. The intercept comes out at 19,052
    against a true 20,000.
  - Contributions sum exactly to the point: passes, by construction. Forecast
    rejects any point that differs from Σ term amounts, and a test checks it
    across 20 seeds.
  - Excluding(COVID) removes exactly Mar 2020 – Dec 2021: passes. Feb 2020 and
    Jan 2022 levels stay, and a separate test pins down that the Jan 2022 change
    is lost.
  - andThen composes in order: passes. One test checks the call order, and
    another that the first window's result feeds the second.
  - No regression row spans the gap: passes. With COVID excluded, AR's rows
    never pair Feb 2020 with anything after the gap; Feb–Apr 2022 drop out and
    rows resume at May 2022. A mutation check confirmed it: when I turned off
    the lag check inside a throwaway container, this test failed.

  What was added
  - Domain:
    - MonthRange.
    - Explanation with the sealed Term (Constant, Weighted).
    - Forecast.
    - PrintHistory.excluding(range).
  - Forecast layer:
    - The Forecaster interface, NaiveForecaster and AutoregressiveForecaster(p),
      fitted by OLS on each call.
    - HistoryWindow with andThen, AllHistory and Excluding.
    - InsufficientHistoryException.
  - Config: p comes from adp.ar.order=3. Beans are registered as naive and ar
    for models and all and exclude-covid for windows, the names P5's CLI will
    accept. The COVID range lives in ForecastConfig.

  How your notes were recorded
  - The months-before-target rule: AR throws InsufficientHistoryException when
    any of the p months before the target is missing, naming the first missing
    month, while naive still forecasts. A test covers a history ending Feb 2022.
  - DESIGN.md:
    - D4: the term-shape Explanation and the exception.
    - D5: your P4 rule: score only months where every compared model forecast,
      and report how many were skipped.
    - D6: the lost Jan 2022 change and consecutive lags.
    - D8: your P5 follow-up, largest-remainder rounding so displayed rows add up
      to the displayed total.
    - D7 and the CLAUDE.md pattern map: MonthRange, Forecast and Explanation are
      listed as value objects, and "one explanation type per model" is under
      "deliberately not used".

  Two choices you didn't make explicitly
  - When the fit fails: if the lags are collinear (for example, a flat series),
    AR throws IllegalStateException rather than InsufficientHistoryException.
    P4's backtest will therefore treat that as a real failure, not a skipped
    month.
  - Naive and the exception: naive also throws InsufficientHistoryException when
    the latest month has no change. In practice that means a history of a
    single month.

  Should I commit this as P3?

❯ Read P4 in PROMPTS-PLAN.md and answer its open questions first                

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● I've read P4. Here are my answers to its two open questions, plus two points
  P4 doesn't raise but needs settled before coding.

  Q1 — Template Method backtester or composition?

  Composition, as the pattern map says. I don't disagree. There is a real case
  for Template Method, though, and it's not hypothetical: we now have two
  backtests, the real-time headline and the latest-vintage secondary. They share
  the walk-forward loop and differ in two steps: what history the model sees at
  each target, and what counts as the truth. That is the textbook shape for an
  abstract Backtester with two subclasses.

  Those two steps are differences in data, not in algorithm. The latest-vintage
  backtest is just the real-time backtest run on a history in which every month
  has a single vintage holding its latest level. I'd add one domain method that
  builds that view:

  - PrintHistory.latestOnly() gives each month one observation: its latest
    level, stamped with a synthetic release date of the first of the following
    month.
  - What the model sees: at each target M, asOf(day before M's release) then
    contains every earlier month at its revised value. That's exactly the biased
    fit D2 describes.
  - What counts as the truth: firstPrintChange(M) becomes the latest-vintage
    change, again matching D2.
  - Why a synthetic date is needed: in the real data, all backcast months share
    the 2022-08-31 vintage, so their true dates would make asOf empty for every
    target before 2022.

  So there is one Backtester and one loop, run on history for the headline and
  on history.latestOnly() for the secondary. The difference between the two
  backtests is visible in a single domain method, rather than spread across
  overridden hooks.

  Q2 — Should the scoring window match the fit window, or be independent?

  Independent, as D6 already says. Tying them together breaks the comparison
  table. With fit=all the table would also score the COVID months; with
  fit=exclude-covid it would skip them. exclude-covid would then "win" largely
  because it avoided the hardest months, not because it forecast better, and the
  table would mix two effects into one number.

  Two rules follow from that:
  - The scoring window stays fixed across every row of a comparison table. Only
    the fit window varies, so any difference between rows comes from the fit.
  - Every cell is scored on the same months, not just every model within a row.
    This extends your D5 rule. Each scored month must be one that every model
    forecast under every fit window in the table, and the table reports how many
    months were skipped. Otherwise, AR under exclude-covid can't forecast
    Feb–Apr 2022 while AR under all can, and the rows would quietly be scored on
    different months.

  How each backtest uses it:
  - Real-time headline: the scoring window has no effect, because its targets
    (Sep 2022 onward) contain no COVID months. The table varies only the fit
    window.
  - Latest-vintage secondary: this is where your "COVID scoring question" lives.
    I'd report a 2×2: fit {all, exclude-covid} × scoring {all, exclude-covid}.

  Mechanics: the scoring window reuses HistoryWindow, so no new type is needed.
  Target M is scored if M still has a level after scoringWindow.apply(history).
  The truth always comes from the unwindowed history.

  Two points P4 doesn't raise

  1. Which months are targets at all. Backcast months (2010 to Aug 2022) all
  first appear in the 2022-08-31 vintage. For any of them, asOf(2022-08-30) is
  empty, so every model throws InsufficientHistoryException. Counting all of
  them as "skipped" would report about 150 skips that are really "this month was
  never forecastable in real time".

  I'd define the target set explicitly: M is a target if, the day before M's 
  first release, the latest month held is M−1. In other words, the forecast made
  at that moment is for M. Backcast months aren't targets in the real-time
  backtest, and "skipped" then means only "a model couldn't forecast a month it
  should have". As a sanity check, the backtester asserts that each forecast's
  target equals M.

  2. The AccuracyMetric signature. Skill vs. naive needs the baseline's errors
  as well as the model's, so a metric over one forecaster's results doesn't fit.
  I'd make:
  - the backtester always run naive as the baseline alongside the model, on the
    same months;
  - its output a list of rows (target, firstPrint, modelPoint, naivePoint);
  - the metric double compute(List<Row>):
    - MAE ignores the naive column.
    - Skill = 1 − MAE_model / MAE_naive, which is undefined (reported as such)
      when MAE_naive = 0.

  With that shape, "naive skill vs naive = 0" holds by construction.

  Metrics compute in whole jobs, and the CLI renders thousands (K). The ±15%
  sampling-noise figure stays README text, as you decided; computing it would be
  interval work, which P4 lists as out of scope.

  Decisions I need:
  1. Q1: composition, with the latest-vintage backtest done through
     PrintHistory.latestOnly() rather than a second backtester class?
  2. Q2: independent windows, the scoring window fixed per table, every cell
     scored on the same months, and the 2×2 for the secondary?
  3. Target set: "M is a target if M−1 is the latest month held the day before
     M's release"?
  4. Metric shape: naive always runs as the baseline, and AccuracyMetric
     computes over (target, firstPrint, modelPoint, naivePoint) rows?

● All four are decided. Making "the real-time backtest never receives a
  latestOnly() history" testable needs one more piece. A latestOnly() history
  will carry a marker, isLatestOnly(), which asOf and excluding preserve. The
  backtester takes an explicit Basis (REAL_TIME or LATEST_VINTAGE):
  - REAL_TIME rejects a latestOnly history outright.
  - LATEST_VINTAGE builds history.latestOnly() itself.

  A spy then confirms that no real-time forecast call ever sees the marker.

  I'm keeping the class name SkillVsNaive from the pattern map, even though the
  baseline is now injectable. Reports name the baseline actually used.

      19 +    return month.next().firstDay().plusDays(4);                
      20 +  }

         +firstPrints, long revision) {

      25 +    var base = first.previous();                               
      26 +    long level = 100_000_000;                                  
      27 +    observations.add(new Observation(base, releaseOf(base), lev
         +el));

         +nth), revised));                                               
      36 +      }                                                        
      37 +      level = revised + change;

         + level));

  ⎿  Created src/main/java/adp/evaluation/SkillVsNaive.java (+29 -0)
       1 +package adp.evaluation;                                        
       2 +                                                               
       3 +import java.util.List;                                         
       4 +import java.util.OptionalDouble;                               
       5 +                                                               
       6 +/**                                                            
       7 + * {@code 1 − MAE_model / MAE_baseline}: positive means the mod
         +el beats the baseline. The baseline is                         
       8 + * whatever the backtester was given (naive by default). Undefi
         +ned when the baseline is perfect.                              
       9 + */                                                            
      10 +public class SkillVsNaive implements AccuracyMetric {          
      11 +                                                               
      12 +  @Override                                                    
      13 +  public String name() {                                       
      14 +    return "skill";                                            
      15 +  }                                                            
      16 +                                                               
      17 +  @Override                                                    
      18 +  public OptionalDouble compute(List<ScoredMonth> rows) {      
      19 +    if (rows.isEmpty()) {                                      
      20 +      return OptionalDouble.empty();                           
      21 +    }                                                          
      22 +    double model = rows.stream().mapToLong(r -> Math.abs(r.mode
         +lError())).sum();                                              
      23 +    double baseline = rows.stream().mapToLong(r -> Math.abs(r.b
         +aselineError())).sum();                                        
      24 +    if (baseline == 0) {                                       
      25 +      return OptionalDouble.empty();                           
      26 +    }                                                          
      27 +    return OptionalDouble.of(1 - model / baseline);            
      28 +  }                                                            
      29 +}

● All tests pass on the first run, so I'll verify the key tests actually catch
  failures by deliberately introducing two bugs—a look-ahead leak and a removed
  basis guard—and confirming the corresponding tests fail as expected, while

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both mutations break the build, but mutation 1 is too blunt—it also disrupts
  target selection, muddying whether the failure stems from the vintage check
  itself. I'll craft a sharper mutation that preserves correct target selection
  while only leaking the M−1 revision published on M's release day, isolating
  the realistic look-ahead bug.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● I found that the look-ahead guard alone fails against the vintage assertion,
  catching a subtle same-day leak rather than a broken loop—and the gap test
  revealed that under fit=exclude-covid, COVID months get excluded from every
  cell in the 2×2 due to the "same months" rule, leaving only the fit=all row
  able to surface the dropped months. I'll log this in D5 without changing the
  rule, then move on to the docs.

● Update(docs/DESIGN.md)
  ⎿  Added 18 lines
      124    report how many were skipped. After an excluded window, AR c
           annot forecast the first p months while naive
      125    can; scoring them for naive alone would make the two MAEs in
           comparable.
      126  
      127 +**Backtest mechanics (P4):**                                  
      128 +                                                              
      129 +- One `Backtester(model, baseline, fitWindow, metrics)`; the b
          +aseline is injected (naive by default). Each                  
      130 +  scored month is a row `(target, firstPrint, modelPoint, base
          +linePoint)`; `AccuracyMetric` computes over rows.             
      131 +- **Targets:** M is a target if, the day before M's first rele
          +ase, the latest month held is M−1. Months first               
      132 +  published together (the 2022 relaunch backcast) are never ta
          +rgets, so they don't count as skipped. A                      
      133 +  forecast for any month other than M is an error.            
      134 +- **Basis:** `REAL_TIME` (headline) runs on real vintages and 
          +rejects a `latestOnly()` history.                             
      135 +  `LATEST_VINTAGE` (secondary, biased) runs the same loop on `
          +PrintHistory.latestOnly()`: each month's latest               
      136 +  level stamped with a *synthetic* release date (1st of the fo
          +llowing month).                                               
      137 +- **Windows are independent.** In a comparison table the scori
          +ng window is fixed and only the fit window                    
      138 +  varies, and every cell is scored on the same months: those e
          +very cell's model and baseline forecast. A                    
      139 +  cell whose fit window removed the month before the target ca
          +n't forecast it. Each report line shows `n`                   
      140 +  (scored months) next to MAE and skill, plus the skipped coun
          +t.                                                            
      141 +- **Consequence for the 2×2:** `fit=exclude-covid` cannot fore
          +cast COVID-period targets, so in a table that                 
      142 +  includes it those months drop out for every cell (visible in
          + the skipped count). The COVID scoring question               
      143 +  is answered by the `fit=all` row under both scoring windows.
      144 +                                                              
      145  **Correction recorded:** the backtest *evaluates*; it does not
            train. The model fits itself (OLS) inside each
      146  `forecast()` call. Using backtest scores to pick `p` or a wind
           ow would be model selection and would bias the
      147  reported score.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

● P4 is done. make test and make lint both pass, with 97 tests in total. Nothing
  is committed yet.

  Your stop criteria
  - Look-ahead guard: passes. Spies on both the model and the baseline check
    that no forecaster ever sees a vintage on or after the release it's
    predicting. They also confirm the real-time backtest never hands a
    forecaster a latestOnly() history. Three planted bugs, each run in a
    throwaway container, made it fail:
    - forecasting from asOf(release) instead of the day before;
    - a subtle leak of only the M−1 revision published on M's release day;
    - removing the real-time backtest's rejection of latestOnly() histories.
  - Naive skill vs. naive = 0: passes, exactly 0.0, with n = 39.
  - MAE on a hand-computed example: passes. First prints of 100/150/120/180/160K
    give errors of 50/30/60/20K, so MAE = 40K with n = 4, even with revisions
    in the data.

  Your four decisions, as implemented
  - Composition:
    - PrintHistory.latestOnly() builds the secondary's history. Its Javadoc
      states plainly that its release dates are synthetic (the 1st of the
      following month).
    - A latestOnly() history carries a marker that asOf and excluding preserve.
    - Basis.REAL_TIME rejects such a history; LATEST_VINTAGE builds it itself.
  - Independent windows:
    - Backtester.compare holds the scoring window fixed and scores every cell on
      the same months.
    - Each BacktestReport carries n(), skipped, the model, baseline, window
      names and basis, so P5 can print n next to MAE and skill.
  - Target set: M is a target only if M−1 was the latest month held the day
    before M's release. Relaunch-style batches aren't targets and aren't counted
    as skips. A forecast for the wrong month throws.
  - Metrics: each row is (target, firstPrint, modelPoint, baselinePoint), and
    the baseline is injected rather than hard-wired. I kept the class name
    SkillVsNaive from the pattern map, but its report label is just "skill", and
    the report names the baseline actually used.

  One rule I had to add. When a fit window removes the month just before a
  target, that cell can't forecast the target, so it counts as a skip rather
  than an error.

  This changes your 2×2. Under fit=exclude-covid, COVID-period targets can't be
  forecast at all. By the same-months rule, they then drop out of every cell in
  any table that includes that fit window. So "fit exclude-covid × score all"
  ends up identical to "fit exclude-covid × score exclude-covid". The COVID
  scoring question is answered by the fit=all row under both scoring windows,
  and the skipped count shows the dropped months.

  I recorded this in D5 along with the backtest mechanics. If you want the full
  2×2 to be meaningful, the alternative is to score each scoring window's table
  with only the fit=all cell.

  Docs
  - D5: baseline injection, the target rule, the Basis/latestOnly mechanics, the
    independent windows and same-months scoring, n on every line, and the 2×2
    consequence above.
  - D7 and the pattern map: the Evaluation row is now "Strategy + composition",
    listing Backtester(model, baseline, fitWindow, metrics) and
    PrintHistory.latestOnly() for the biased basis.

  Should I commit this as P4?

❯ Read P5 in PROMPTS-PLAN.md and answer its open questions first                

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● I've read P5. Here are my answers to its two open questions, plus three gaps
  between the prompt and what we've decided since. They need settling before I
  code.

  Q1 — Is a Renderer interface warranted yet?

  No. I agree with the pattern map. The strongest case for one:
  - Testability: rendering is now non-trivial (largest-remainder rounding, K
    formatting, padded signs) and deserves its own unit tests.
  - A real second consumer: both forecast --explain and backtest print the same
    metric line.

  Neither argument needs an interface. Both are met by one concrete class in
  cli/ (TextRenderer) that the commands call and tests exercise directly. An
  interface with one implementation would just be a placeholder for JSON output,
  which P5 lists as out of scope. When a second format arrives, extracting the
  interface from that class is a mechanical refactor. I'd keep "Renderer
  interface (one format)" in the map's "deliberately not used" column.

  Q2 — Should forecast default to AR or naive?

  AR(3). Three reasons:
  - Design: AR is the model the tool exists to provide. Naive is the yardstick
    for it.
  - Explanation: naive's explanation is one line ("1.00 × Sep"). The tool's
    third job, explaining why, only has substance with AR.
  - Honest selection: picking the default by whichever scored better on the
    backtest would be model selection on the test set, which D5 rules out. The
    default has to come from the design, not the score.

  The honesty safeguard is D8's last line, which always shows the backtest
  skill. I'd add one thing: if skill ≤ 0, print a note under it, such as note: 
  naive has been at least as accurate over these n months. A default user then
  can't miss it if AR isn't earning its keep. P6 Q2 asks exactly that question
  of the real data.

  Three gaps to settle

  1. The acceptance test can't pass on the current sample data. "forecast 
  --explain against the bundled snapshot" means the 12-row sample: 6 months and
  5 changes, which is only 2 complete AR(3) rows. AR needs more than 4, and the
  trust line needs real-time targets. Options:
  - (a) Recommended: grow the sample to about 40 months. It would still be
    hand-made and still labelled SAMPLE DATA in the file name and header, and
    I'd generate it once from a fixed seed. The test checks only the shape of
    D8's output, never numbers, so it keeps passing unchanged when P6 swaps in
    the real snapshot.
  - (b) Test-only fixture. Point the @SpringBootTest at a fixture in
    src/test/resources instead. The sample stays at 12 rows, but the test
    wouldn't literally run "against the bundled snapshot".

  Either way, the test seeds a temporary database through sync --source csv and
  then runs forecast --explain.

  2. backtest needs two options the prompt doesn't list. Your D2 and D5
  decisions need a basis and a scoring window to run the headline, the secondary
  and the 2×2:
  - --basis real-time|latest-vintage, default real-time.
  - --score <window>, default all, fixed for the whole table, per D5.

  Proposed output:
  AR(3) vs naive · real-time first prints · scored on all
  fit window      n    MAE   skill
  all            49    61K   +0.18
  exclude-covid  49    58K   +0.22
  skipped: 0 of 49 targets

  3. The history output isn't specified anywhere. My proposal:
  - Default: the last N months (default 12), each with its latest level and
    latest change.
  - --first-print: adds two columns, the first-print change and the revision
    (latest − first). That makes ADP's revisions visible, which is D1's whole
    point.

  Details I'd settle myself unless you object:
  - Trust line, uses the headline backtest: real-time basis, the same fit window
    as the forecast, and n shown. When the model is naive, skill is omitted (it
    would always be 0). With too few targets, the line reads backtest: not 
    enough history.
  - Trust line, cost: the backtest runs live on every forecast call, as D8 says.
    That's fine at this scale.
  - Staleness warning: goes to stderr, e.g. warning: newest vintage is 
    2026-08-05 (78 days old); run make init or sync. "Today" comes from an
    injected Clock bean so tests can control it.
  - Month labels: Oct 2026 and Sep are formatted with Locale.US explicitly, so
    the output doesn't change with the machine's locale.
  - Error messages: unknown names list the valid ones, as sync already does.
    InsufficientHistoryException prints as a one-line error.

  Decisions I need:
  1. Q1: no Renderer interface, just one concrete TextRenderer?
  2. Q2: default to AR, with the skill ≤ 0 note?
  3. Sample data: (a) or (b)?
  4. --basis and --score on backtest?
  5. The history columns as proposed?

      12 +/** Loads everything stored and warns on stderr when the newest
         + vintage is stale. */                                          
      13 +final class StoredHistory {                                    
      14 +                                                               
      15 +  static final long STALE_AFTER_DAYS = 35;                     
      16 +                                                               
      17 +  private StoredHistory() {}                                   
      18 +                                                               
      19 +  static PrintHistory load(PrintRepository repository, Clock cl
         +ock, PrintWriter err) {                                        
      20 +    var observations = repository.findAll();                   
      21 +    if (observations.isEmpty()) {                              
      22 +      throw new IllegalStateException("no data stored; run make
         + init or adp sync");                                           
      23 +    }                                                          
      24 +    LocalDate newest =                                         
      25 +        observations.stream().map(Observation::vintageDate).max
         +(Comparator.naturalOrder()).get();                             
      26 +    long age = ChronoUnit.DAYS.between(newest, LocalDate.now(cl
         +ock));                                                         
      27 +    if (age > STALE_AFTER_DAYS) {                              
      28 +      err.println(                                             
      29 +          "warning: newest vintage is " + newest + " (" + age +
         + " days old); run adp sync");                                  
      30 +    }                                                          
      31 +    return new PrintHistory(observations);                     
      32 +  }                                                            
      33 +}

  commands, and nothing is committed yet. I followed your mid-turn decisions. I

  Acceptance
  - The @SpringBootTest smoke test passes. It starts the full app, seeds a
    temporary database with sync, then runs forecast --explain. It checks every
    line of D8's shape, and that the displayed rows add up to the displayed
    total and the headline.
  - The test data is a synthetic series in
    src/test/resources/cli/test_vintages.csv: seeded, 60 months dated 2030–2034,
    a few revisions, labelled TEST DATA in its header.
  - The test counts are confirmed: 25 new CLI tests, all of which ran.

  What it prints, from the real Docker image on that test series:
  Jan 2035 forecast (AR(3), window=exclude-covid): +91K
    intercept                     +86K
    0.00 × Dec (+ 77K)             +0K
    0.10 × Nov (+ 48K)             +5K
    0.00 × Oct (+ 54K)             +0K
    ──────────────────────────────────
    forecast                      +91K
    backtest MAE 49K | skill vs naive +0.17 | n=51
  The weights are near zero because the test series is white noise around +100K,
  so AR is mostly the intercept. That's the correct fit for this data. backtest
  prints the proposed table with n, MAE, skill and skipped: 8 of 59 targets,
  and --basis latest-vintage is labelled "(biased)".

  Your decisions, as implemented
  1. Rendering: one concrete TextRenderer, no interface. It uses
     largest-remainder rounding, with a test where independent rounding would
     show 33 against a total of 32.
  2. Default model: AR. Under the trust line it prints note: naive has been at 
     least as accurate over these n months when skill ≤ 0.
  3. Test data: the docs now match:
     - P5's acceptance wording in PROMPTS-PLAN.md, with a note on why it was
       edited.
     - A P6 follow-up: a second smoke test that runs forecast --explain on the
       real data/adp_vintages.csv.
     - D10's CLI row.
  4. backtest options: --basis real-time|latest-vintage and --score <window>.
     The fit window is repeatable and defaults to all and exclude-covid.
  5. history: level and change by default; --first-print adds the first-print
     and revision columns.

  Other details
  - Unknown names: they list the valid ones and exit with code 2. I fixed a
    "valid basiss" message my own test had enshrined; it now reads "valid
    bases".
  - Staleness warning: goes to stderr when the newest vintage is more than 35
    days old, using an injected Clock. An empty database gives error: no data 
    stored; run make init or adp sync.
  - The baseline name comes from adp.baseline=naive.
  - Accuracy metrics are registered in a new EvaluationConfig, in report order.
  - Backtester is built inside the commands, because the model and window are
    only known at runtime. Which implementations exist is still decided only in
    config/.

  brings the real snapshot.

● Fetch(https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/ADPMNUSNERSA)
  ⎿  Received 74.4KB (200 OK)

● I can't answer P6's questions with results until the real data exists. What I
  can do now is fix how we'll read the results, so the README can't be argued
  backwards from whichever numbers come out. I also found one licensing point.

  Licensing — committing the snapshot

  FRED tags ADPMNUSNERSA as "Copyrighted: Citation Required". The page warns
  that the data is copyrighted and gives a suggested citation:

  ▎ Automatic Data Processing, Inc., Total Nonfarm Private Payroll Employment 
  ▎ [ADPMNUSNERSA], retrieved from FRED, Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis

  As I understand FRED's tags, this one means you may share the data as long as
  it's cited. The stricter tag is "pre-approval required", which this series
  doesn't have. So committing data/adp_vintages.csv should be fine as long as
  the citation goes in the CSV's header comment and in the README. That's my
  reading of FRED's labelling, not legal advice. If the repo will be public and
  you want certainty, check FRED's legal page.

  Q1 — Do the results support the exclude-covid default?

  What I can say before running anything:
  - The headline backtest can't measure COVID scoring. Its targets start in Sep
    2022, so --score has no effect there. It can only measure the COVID fit
    effect, i.e. whether training AR on 2020–21 hurts its forecasts in 2022–26.
  - Naive is identical in both rows. It only uses the last change, so any
    difference between the all and exclude-covid rows is AR's alone.
  - Expectation: exclude-covid should help AR. The 2020 swings in the backcast
    are millions of jobs, and OLS is driven by squared errors, so with fit=all
    those months dominate the coefficients. I'm labelling this an expectation,
    not a result.

  The rule I propose to fix now, before seeing the numbers:
  - The default stays exclude-covid whatever the table shows. D6 chose it on
    domain grounds (a structural break), and switching to whichever row scored
    better would be model selection on the test set, which D5 forbids.
  - The README reports the comparison honestly either way. If fit=all scores
    better, it says so and explains why we keep the default anyway.
  - Small differences count as no difference. With about 49 targets and ±15%
    sampling noise, an MAE gap under about 15%, or a skill gap under about 0.1,
    gets reported as "no measurable difference", not as a win for either window.
  - The COVID scoring question goes to the secondary backtest. That's the
    fit=all row under both scoring windows, labelled biased (per D5).

  Q2 — Does AR(3) actually beat naive?

  Expectation: probably yes, but for an unglamorous reason.
  - Why naive is a weak bar here: monthly ADP changes are noisy, with weak
    persistence. Repeating last month's change doubles the noise variance
    compared with forecasting the mean, so on white noise naive's skill gap is
    large. Our synthetic test series showed the effect: skill +0.17 with AR
    weights near zero.
  - So AR can "beat naive" just by being an intercept. It would effectively be
    forecasting the recent mean, not exploiting dynamics.

  How the README should interpret each outcome:
  - Skill > 0 with small AR weights: say plainly that the gain comes from mean
    reversion, that a constant-mean model would likely do about as well, and
    that the --explain output shows it (the intercept dominates). Adding a mean
    baseline goes on the "next week" list.
  - Skill > 0 with meaningful weights: report it, with n and the noise caveat.
  - Skill ≤ 0: say so directly. It's still a useful finding for three reasons:
    - It shows the backtest is honest. No tuning on the test set; p was fixed up
      front.
    - It shows the safeguard works. The trust note warns default users
      automatically.
    - It tells the reader something real. Over this window, ADP's last print is
      as good a guide as its own short history. That argues for exogenous
      predictors (BLS, claims), which are already on the next-week list.

  What I need to proceed

  1. A FRED API key. Free at fred.stlouisfed.org/docs/api/api_key.html. Export
     it as FRED_API_KEY in the shell where you run make, or run ! export 
     FRED_API_KEY=… in this session. It is the gate for everything else in P6:
     - the snapshot via make refresh-snapshot;
     - the first live FredSource run and the exact target count for D2;
     - the backtest numbers and the README.
  2. Agree the pre-registered rules above:
     - the default stays exclude-covid regardless of results;
     - differences inside the noise band are reported as no measurable
       difference;
     - the interpretation rule for each AR-vs-naive outcome.
  3. Confirm the citation approach: the citation goes in the CSV header and the
     README, and the snapshot gets committed.
