● Write(README.md)
  ⎿  Added 203 lines, removed 1 line
        1  # adp-forecast
        2 -adp-forecast                                                  
        2 +                                                              
        3 +A command-line tool that tracks the monthly ADP National Emplo
          +yment Report (total private payrolls) and                     
        4 +forecasts the next print. It answers three questions:         
        5 +                                                              
        6 +1. **What were the numbers?** `history` shows each month as cu
          +rrently published, and with `--first-print`                   
        7 +   what the market first saw and how much it has been revised 
          +since.                                                        
        8 +2. **What comes next?** `forecast` predicts next month's chang
          +e.                                                            
        9 +3. **Why, and how far to trust it?** `forecast --explain` brea
          +ks the forecast into its terms and shows the                  
       10 +   model's measured accuracy against first prints.            
       11 +                                                              
       12 +Design decisions and their reasons are in [`docs/DESIGN.md`](d
          +ocs/DESIGN.md). The AI-assisted build is                      
       13 +documented in [`docs/PROMPTS-PLAN.md`](docs/PROMPTS-PLAN.md) a
          +nd [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md).                                  
       14 +                                                              
       15 +## Quick start                                                
       16 +                                                              
       17 +Requires Docker and `make`. No API key needed: the repository 
          +ships a snapshot of every published vintage.                  
       18 +                                                              
       19 +```                                                           
       20 +make init                                    # build the image
          +, seed the database from the snapshot                         
       21 +make run ARGS="forecast --explain"                            
       22 +```                                                           
       23 +                                                              
       24 +```                                                           
       25 +Oct 2026 forecast (AR(3), window=exclude-covid): +112K        
       26 +  intercept                     +48K                          
       27 +  0.71 × Sep (+ 90K)            +64K                          
       28 +  -0.14 × Aug (+ 36K)            -5K                          
       29 +  0.10 × Jul (+ 44K)             +5K                          
       30 +  ──────────────────────────────────                          
       31 +  forecast                     +112K                          
       32 +  backtest MAE 62K | skill vs naive +0.07 | n=49              
       33 +```                                                           
       34 +                                                              
       35 +The forecast is the sum of the rows: an intercept plus a weigh
          +t times each of the last three published                      
       36 +changes. The last line is a live backtest of exactly this mode
          +l and window: mean absolute error against                     
       37 +first prints over `n` months, and skill relative to the naive 
          +"same as last month" forecast. See                            
       38 +[Results](#results) for what those numbers do and don't suppor
          +t.                                                            
       39 +                                                              
       40 +### Commands                                                  
       41 +                                                              
       42 +| Command | What it does |                                    
       43 +|---|---|                                                     
       44 +| `history [--last N] [--first-print]` | Recent months: level 
          +and change; optionally first print and revision |             
       45 +| `forecast [--model ar\|naive] [--window all\|exclude-covid] 
          +[--explain]` | Next month's forecast; defaults `ar`, `exclude-
          +covid` |                                                      
       46 +| `backtest [--model …] [--window …]… [--score …] [--basis rea
          +l-time\|latest-vintage]` | Accuracy table, one row per fit win
          +dow, all scored on the same months |                          
       47 +| `sync [--source csv\|fred] [--export path]` | Load vintages 
          +into the local database (never overwrites a stored vintage) | 
       48 +                                                              
       49 +Run any of them with `make run ARGS="…"`. Unknown model or win
          +dow names list the valid ones. A warning                      
       50 +appears when the newest stored vintage is more than 35 days ol
          +d.                                                            
       51 +                                                              
       52 +### Live data (optional)                                      
       53 +                                                              
       54 +Put a free [FRED API key](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/docs/api
          +/api_key.html) in a gitignored `.env` file                    
       55 +(`FRED_API_KEY=…`, no quotes). `make init` then also syncs fro
          +m FRED, and `make refresh-snapshot` rewrites                  
       56 +`data/adp_vintages.csv` from the API.                         
       57 +                                                              
       58 +## Data                                                       
       59 +                                                              
       60 +- **Series:** FRED `ADPMNUSNERSA`, total nonfarm private payro
          +ll employment, persons, seasonally adjusted.                  
       61 +  The tool stores levels exactly as published and derives chan
          +ges, first prints and revisions itself.                       
       62 +- **Every vintage:** the snapshot holds all 1,302 values ALFRE
          +D has recorded across 50 publication dates,                   
       63 +  each with the date it was first published. "September's numb
          +er" is ambiguous (first print or revised?),                   
       64 +  so forecasts are judged against the **first print**, and bac
          +ktests never see a value published later                      
       65 +  than the moment being simulated.                            
       66 +- **Coverage starts 2022-08-31.** ADP relaunched the report wi
          +th a new methodology that day. The 2010 – Jul                 
       67 +  2022 history first appears in that vintage as a backcast, so
          + those months never had a real-time first                     
       68 +  print. The full history has since been restated several time
          +s (QCEW reweighting): 2023-02-01,                             
       69 +  2024-01-31, 2025-02-05, 2025-03-05, 2025-04-02 and 2026-02-0
          +4.                                                            
       70 +- **Citation (required by FRED for this copyrighted series):**
          + Automatic Data Processing, Inc., Total                       
       71 +  Nonfarm Private Payroll Employment [ADPMNUSNERSA], retrieved
          + from FRED, Federal Reserve Bank of St.                       
       72 +  Louis. The same citation heads `data/adp_vintages.csv`.     
       73 +                                                              
       74 +## Approach and trade-offs                                    
       75 +                                                              
       76 +- **Two models, one interface.** `naive` (next change = last c
          +hange) is the baseline every score is                         
       77 +  measured against. `ar` is AR(3) on monthly changes, fitted b
          +y ordinary least squares on every call. Its                   
       78 +  forecast decomposes exactly into `intercept + Σ weight × lag
          +`, which is the explanation. Richer models                    
       79 +  were cut on purpose: they would be harder to explain, and th
          +e accuracy method below would be needed to                    
       80 +  justify them anyway.                                        
       81 +- **Explanations sum exactly.** Each term is rounded to whole 
          +jobs and the forecast is defined as their                     
       82 +  sum, so the rows always add up; display rounding to thousand
          +s uses largest-remainder rounding to keep it                  
       83 +  that way.                                                   
       84 +- **COVID is excluded from the fit by default** (Mar 2020 – De
          +c 2021, a structural break). This was decided                 
       85 +  on domain grounds before seeing any results; the backtest sh
          +ows the sensitivity.                                          
       86 +- **The AR order is fixed at 3** and never tuned on the backte
          +st; tuning it there would turn the test set                   
       87 +  into a training set.                                        
       88 +- **Architecture:** ports and adapters. The domain, forecastin
          +g and evaluation code is plain Java with no                   
       89 +  framework; Spring Boot does dependency injection at the edge
          +s only (enforced by an ArchUnit test).                        
       90 +  Adding a model or window is one class plus one `@Bean`. Stor
          +age is SQLite behind a repository interface.                  
       91 +  The pattern map in `CLAUDE.md` lists each pattern used and t
          +he ones deliberately not used.                                
       92 +                                                              
       93 +## How accuracy is measured                                   
       94 +                                                              
       95 +A **walk-forward backtest**. For each target month M:         
       96 +                                                              
       97 +1. Take the history exactly as it stood the day before M was f
          +irst published (`asOf`).                                      
       98 +2. Apply the fit window, let each model fit itself, and foreca
          +st M.                                                         
       99 +3. Compare with M's **first print**.                          
      100 +                                                              
      101 +- **Targets** are months whose predecessor was the newest mont
          +h available at that moment: Sep 2022 – Sep                    
      102 +  2026, **49 months**. Months published together in the relaun
          +ch backcast are not targets.                                  
      103 +- **Like for like:** every model and every fit window in a tab
          +le is scored on the same months; months any                   
      104 +  of them couldn't forecast are counted as skipped.           
      105 +- **Metrics:** mean absolute error (MAE, thousands of jobs) an
          +d skill = `1 − MAE_model / MAE_naive`                         
      106 +  (positive = better than naive).                             
      107 +- **Sampling noise:** with 49 months, an MAE carries roughly ±
          +15% sampling noise, so small differences                      
      108 +  between models or windows are not evidence of anything.     
      109 +- **Secondary backtest (biased):** the same loop on the latest
          + revised values, Feb 2010 – Sep 2026. It has                  
      110 +  ~4× more months and includes COVID, but both the fit and the
          + "truth" are revised data, so it is used                      
      111 +  only for sensitivity checks, never as the headline.         
      112 +                                                              
      113 +## Pre-registered interpretation                              
      114 +                                                              
      115 +Fixed before the backtest was run, so the results can't be rea
          +d to fit them:                                                
      116 +                                                              
      117 +1. **The default window stays `exclude-covid` whatever the res
          +ults show.** It was chosen on domain grounds;                 
      118 +   switching to whichever window scored better would be model 
          +selection on the test set.                                    
      119 +2. **Differences inside the noise band count as no difference:
          +** an MAE gap under ~15% or a skill gap under                 
      120 +   ~0.1 is reported as "no measurable difference", not as a wi
          +n.                                                            
      121 +3. **AR vs. naive:** if AR wins with near-zero weights, it is 
          +only exploiting the mean; if it wins with                     
      122 +   meaningful weights, report it with `n` and the noise caveat
          +; if it doesn't win, say so — the tool then                   
      123 +   tells users itself (`--explain` prints a note when skill ≤ 
          +0).                                                           
      124 +                                                              
      125 +## Results                                                    
      126 +                                                              
      127 +Snapshot retrieved 2026-10-22; newest vintage 2026-09-30.     
      128 +                                                              
      129 +### Headline: real-time first prints                          
      130 +                                                              
      131 +| fit window | n | MAE | skill vs naive |                     
      132 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      133 +| all | 49 | 61K | +0.08 |                                    
      134 +| exclude-covid (default) | 49 | 62K | +0.07 |                
      135 +| *naive baseline* | 49 | 66K | — |                           
      136 +                                                              
      137 +No targets were skipped.                                      
      138 +                                                              
      139 +**Does AR(3) beat naive?** Nominally, by 7–8% of MAE; under ru
          +le 2 that is **no measurable difference**.                    
      140 +AR is not merely its intercept: it puts a weight of 0.71 on la
          +st month's change, so it behaves like a                       
      141 +damped naive forecast pulled toward a long-run level. That is 
          +still a useful finding: over these 49 months,                 
      142 +ADP's own short history adds little beyond the last print, whi
          +ch points to other information (BLS                           
      143 +payrolls, jobless claims) rather than more elaborate models of
          + the same series.                                             
      144 +                                                              
      145 +**Do the results support the `exclude-covid` default?** They n
          +either support nor contradict it: 61K vs.                     
      146 +62K, skill +0.08 vs. +0.07, well inside the noise band. Per ru
          +le 1 the default stays. The window does                       
      147 +change the model, though: fitted on all history, AR's weights 
          +are 1.16 / −0.35 / −0.01 instead of                           
      148 +0.71 / −0.14 / 0.10, and this month's forecast is +120K instea
          +d of +112K. The COVID swings reshape the                      
      149 +coefficients without making the forecasts measurably better or
          + worse over 2022–2026.                                        
      150 +                                                              
      151 +### Secondary: latest vintage (biased)                        
      152 +                                                              
      153 +Scoring window fixed, both fit windows:                       
      154 +                                                              
      155 +| scored on | fit window | n | MAE | skill |                  
      156 +|---|---|---|---|---|                                         
      157 +| all | all | 167 | 56K | −0.01 |                             
      158 +| all | exclude-covid | 167 | 55K | +0.01 |                   
      159 +| exclude-covid | all | 166 | 56K | +0.00 |                   
      160 +| exclude-covid | exclude-covid | 166 | 55K | +0.02 |         
      161 +                                                              
      162 +With `fit=exclude-covid` in the table, COVID-period months can
          +'t be forecast and drop out for every row                     
      163 +(33 and 12 skipped), so these rows say nothing about scoring C
          +OVID. Fit windows again make no measurable                    
      164 +difference, and AR vs. naive is a wash.                       
      165 +                                                              
      166 +**Should COVID months be scored?** Answered by the `fit=all` r
          +ow alone:                                                     
      167 +                                                              
      168 +| scored on | n | MAE | skill |                               
      169 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      170 +| all | 192 | 85K | −0.07 |                                   
      171 +| exclude-covid | 170 | 60K | −0.03 |                         
      172 +                                                              
      173 +The 22 COVID-era months raise MAE from 60K to 85K. Scoring the
          +m would let one extraordinary episode                         
      174 +dominate any accuracy number, which is why the headline (2022 
          +onward) is the right yardstick and COVID is                   
      175 +best reported separately.                                     
      176 +                                                              
      177 +### AR vs. a moving-average baseline                          
      178 +                                                              
      179 +*Pending P7.* The baseline is injectable, so once `MovingAvera
          +geForecaster` exists this section will report                 
      180 +AR's skill against it. That answers directly whether AR adds a
          +nything beyond a smoothed level.                              
      181 +                                                              
      182 +## Next week                                                  
      183 +                                                              
      184 +- **Diebold–Mariano test** for forecast accuracy, replacing th
          +e ±15% / 0.1 noise-band heuristic with a                      
      185 +  proper test of whether two models' errors differ.           
      186 +- **Exogenous predictors** (BLS nonfarm payrolls, jobless clai
          +ms) behind a feature provider — the results                   
      187 +  suggest this is where accuracy would come from.             
      188 +- **Prediction intervals**, with their coverage evaluated in t
          +he same backtest.                                             
      189 +- **Ensemble** weighted by recent error.                      
      190 +- **Selecting `p`** on a held-out earlier window, never on the
          + reported one.                                                
      191 +- Postgres / S3 repositories, Flyway migrations, JSON output, 
          +a small web UI.                                               
      192 +

● The Makefile comment is fixed: it now describes the .env format inline and no
  longer mentions .env.example. Nothing else referenced that file, and Make
  still sees the key. The fix is uncommitted.

  P7 has no open questions

  P7 is the only prompt with no "Open questions" section; it's just a goal and
  an acceptance criterion. Building it raises four decisions, though, and one of
  them collides with the acceptance rule.

  1. The README's "AR vs. moving average" result collides with "no edits outside
  config/". That README section needs AR's skill measured against MA as the
  baseline. My --baseline option for backtest would edit BacktestCommand, an
  existing class outside config/, and fail P7's acceptance.

  Proposal: no class edit. The baseline is already configurable through the
  adp.baseline property, and Spring maps the environment variable ADP_BASELINE
  onto it. make run would pass -e ADP_BASELINE through, a one-line Makefile
  change and not a class. The README result then reproduces as:
  ADP_BASELINE=ma make run ARGS="backtest"
  A --baseline option goes on the "next week" list.

  2. The MA length k must be fixed before seeing results, the same rule as AR's
  p under D5. What each choice answers:
  - k = 3 (recommended): the same three lags AR uses, with equal weights and no
    intercept. AR beating it means fitting the weights and the intercept adds
    something over plain averaging of the same information. It does not fully
    answer "is AR more than its intercept?"; that needs a constant-mean
    baseline, which I'd add under "next week". The README would say so.
  - k = 12: closer to a "smoothed level". It answers a slightly different
    question: does AR beat a slow-moving yearly average?

  3. The Rolling length N must also be fixed in advance.
  - I recommend N = 60 months, registered as the bean rolling-60. It's long
    enough for AR(3) to fit comfortably (about 56 regression rows). It's short
    enough to show D6's bias-variance trade-off against all.
  - What a 60-month window contains: for 2022–2024 targets it still includes
    COVID. That's an honest property of a rolling window, and it composes with
    exclude-covid through andThen if someone wants both.
  - No domain edit needed: Rolling can be built on the existing
    PrintHistory.excluding(MonthRange). Like Excluding, it loses one change at
    its left edge (the first kept month has no prior level), and I'd document
    that.

  4. Where k and N live. They'd be read from adp.ma.window and
  adp.rolling.months. That means adding two small records to AdpProperties,
  which sits in config/ and so is allowed by the acceptance rule. They'd be set
  in application.properties.

  What happens automatically, with no other edits:
  - The CLI: forecast --model ma --explain renders as three Weighted(1/3, …)
    rows, with no renderer change. That's what the term-shape Explanation
    decision in P3 was for.
  - Name lookups: --model and --window list ma and rolling-60 as valid names
    straight away.

  Decisions I need:
  1. Use the ADP_BASELINE environment variable through make run instead of a
     --baseline option?
  2. k = 3 (recommended) or k = 12?
  3. N = 60 for Rolling?
  4. The Makefile comment fix: its own small commit now, or fold it into the P7
     commit?

  ⎿  Created src/main/java/adp/forecast/Rolling.java (+32 -0)
       1 +package adp.forecast;                                          
       2 +                                                               
       3 +import adp.domain.MonthRange;                                  
       4 +import adp.domain.PrintHistory;                                
       5 +import adp.domain.ReferenceMonth;                              
       6 +                                                               
       7 +/**                                                            
       8 + * Keeps the last {@code months} reference months, counted back
         + from the latest month held, so the                            
       9 + * window slides with the as-of date. The first kept month has 
         +no prior level, so the change series                           
      10 + * holds {@code months - 1} changes.                           
      11 + */                                                            
      12 +public record Rolling(int months) implements HistoryWindow {   
      13 +                                                               
      14 +  public Rolling {                                             
      15 +    if (months < 1) {                                          
      16 +      throw new IllegalArgumentException("rolling window must b
         +e at least 1 month: " + months);                               
      17 +    }                                                          
      18 +  }                                                            
      19 +                                                               
      20 +  @Override                                                    
      21 +  public PrintHistory apply(PrintHistory history) {            
      22 +    var levels = history.latest();                             
      23 +    if (levels.isEmpty()) {                                    
      24 +      return history;                                          
      25 +    }                                                          
      26 +    var firstKept = new ReferenceMonth(levels.lastKey().value()
         +.minusMonths(months - 1));                                     
      27 +    if (levels.firstKey().compareTo(firstKept) >= 0) {         
      28 +      return history;                                          
      29 +    }                                                          
      30 +    return history.excluding(new MonthRange(levels.firstKey(), 
         +firstKept.previous()));                                        
      31 +  }                                                            
      32 +}

      73  
      74  ## Approach and trade-offs
      75  
      76 +- **Adding a plug-in is one class plus one `@Bean`.** `ma` (MA(
         +3), equal-weighted mean of the last                            
      77 +  three changes) and the `rolling-60` fit window were added thi
         +s way, with no edits outside `config/`.                        
      78  - **Two models, one interface.** `naive` (next change = last ch
          ange) is the baseline every score is
      79    measured against. `ar` is AR(3) on monthly changes, fitted by
           ordinary least squares on every call. Its
      80    forecast decomposes exactly into `intercept + Σ weight × lag`
          , which is the explanation. Richer models
     ...
      189    suggest this is where accuracy would come from.
      190  - **Prediction intervals**, with their coverage evaluated in t
           he same backtest.
      191  - **Ensemble** weighted by recent error.
      192 +- **Constant-mean baseline**, to answer directly whether AR is
          + more than its intercept (MA(3) answers a                     
      193 +  narrower question: fitted weights vs. equal weights on the s
          +ame lags).                                                    
      194  - **Selecting `p`** on a held-out earlier window, never on the
            reported one.
      195  - Postgres / S3 repositories, Flyway migrations, JSON output, 
           a small web UI.
      196

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The greps confirm several things are fine. Every dependency points inward: the
  domain imports nothing, cli never imports adapter, and Spring only appears in
  adapter, cli and config. They also back up the findings below. Here is the P8
  review, most important first. Nothing has been fixed.

  Violations and gaps

  1. PROMPTS.md doesn't exist (gap, D11; the most important). D11 makes the AI
  workflow a deliverable: each prompt as sent, the raw session log, and a
  used/edited/rejected note. Two things depend on it:
  - The pre-registration claim. The README's "Pre-registered interpretation" was
    agreed before the results in this session, but it was committed in the same
    commit as them. Git can't show the order; only the session log can.
  - The design decisions, such as dropping Print, the latestOnly approach and
    the sample-data choice, which are only argued in the session.

  Recommend: fix. Assembling it is yours, since it's your session log; I can
  draft the notes for each phase.

  2. The "±15% sampling noise" figure was never measured (an honesty gap in the
  README). It's an estimate I gave at P2, and the README states it as fact.
  Pre-registered rule 2 is also defined in terms of it. A quick check is
  possible: the standard error of MAE, computed from the 49 actual absolute
  errors. If it comes out much higher or lower than 15%, the verdicts that
  depend on it (MA vs naive at +0.11, for instance) could change. Recommend: fix
  by measuring and correcting the README, without changing the rule.

  3. The main architecture rule isn't enforced (gap). CLAUDE.md's first rule is
  "dependencies point inward", but ArchitectureTest has a single rule, no Spring
  in the core. Today's code complies, but domain → forecast or forecast → 
  evaluation would pass the build. Recommend: fix with an ArchUnit 
  layeredArchitecture() rule. The "Architecture rules" row already exists in the
  pattern map.

  4. cli/ constructs concrete classes (violates "concrete classes are chosen
  only in config/"):
  - ForecastCommand:87–88 and BacktestCommand:96–105 call new Backtester(…) and
    new NamedWindow(…).
  - ForecastCommand also passes an inline identity window, h -> h, instead of
    the registered all bean.

  These classes are assembled at runtime from names, so the choice of
  implementation still happens in config. But it's undocumented, and the lambda
  duplicates AllHistory. Recommend: fix the lambda (look up all) and record the 
  exception in CLAUDE.md, rather than adding a factory bean (over-engineering 
  for two call sites).

  5. The FRED fixture is still hand-written (D10 says "recorded JSON"). It
  already missed the .0 format once, which broke the first live run. Now that a
  key exists, a trimmed real response can be recorded. Recommend: fix, after 
  checking the response for the key: FRED doesn't echo it, but I'd verify that 
  before committing.

  6. rolling-60 hardcodes 60 in the bean name while N comes from
  adp.rolling.months (ForecastConfig:49). Changing the property would leave the
  name lying. Recommend: fix by deriving the name from the property, or drop the
  property and make 60 a constant. The second is simpler, since the length is 
  pre-registered and shouldn't be tuned anyway.

  Over-engineering and design debt

  7. PrintHistory.isLatestOnly() puts an evaluation concern into the domain. The
  flag only exists so the backtester can refuse a biased history. A domain
  value now carries a marker about how the backtest uses it. The alternative is
  to keep it in evaluation/, for example a LatestVintage wrapper or a method on
  the Basis enum doing the conversion, so the domain has no flag. Recommend: 
  reject for now. It's small, it's tested, and it gives the guarantee you asked
  for at P4. Worth a line in DESIGN.md as accepted debt.

  8. The renderer hardcodes metric names (TextRenderer:122–123, 163–172 look up
  "MAE" and "skill" by string). AccuracyMetric is a strategy, but adding a third
  metric wouldn't show up anywhere, and renaming one silently prints "n/a".
  Recommend: reject as over-engineering to generalise for two metrics. But make 
  the failure loud: use typed lookup, or fail when a metric is missing.

  9. SkillVsNaive is now misnamed. Since --baseline, it measures skill against
  any model. Recommend: rename to SkillVsBaseline and update the pattern map. 
  This is a cheap fix.

  10. Spring Boot for this CLI (P0's question). Spring now does real work: the
  plug-in maps proved P7 needed zero non-config edits, and it binds the
  configuration properties. But the cost shows too: every command opens SQLite
  at startup, and --help needs a writable ADP_DATA_DIR. Recommend: reject, since
  the trade-off is already written down at P0. Note the startup and 
  database-on---help costs in the README's development notes.

  Minor

  - forecast has no --baseline, so its trust line always uses adp.baseline.
    That's inconsistent with backtest. Recommend: fix if cheap, or note it.
  - The runtime container runs as root. Recommend: reject for a local CLI, and 
    note it.
  - D8's "the backtest runs live in milliseconds" was never measured. JVM
    startup dominates the runtime. Recommend: measure it or soften the wording.

  Which do you want fixed? My recommendation is 1–6 and 9, plus the --baseline
  on forecast, each recorded in PROMPTS.md as fixed or rejected with the reason.
  For #1, I'd draft and you'd assemble the raw logs.

       1 +package adp.evaluation;                                        
       2 +                                                               
       3 +import java.util.List;                                         
       4 +import java.util.OptionalDouble;                               
       5 +                                                               
       6 +/**                                                            
       7 + * {@code 1 − MAE_model / MAE_baseline}: positive means the mod
         +el beats the baseline. The baseline                            
       8 + * is whatever the backtester was given (naive by default). Und
         +efined when the baseline is perfect.                           
       9 + */                                                            
      10 +public class SkillVsBaseline implements AccuracyMetric {       
      11 +                                                               
      12 +  @Override                                                    
      13 +  public String name() {                                       
      14 +    return "skill";                                            
      15 +  }                                                            
      16 +                                                               
      17 +  @Override                                                    
      18 +  public OptionalDouble compute(List<ScoredMonth> rows) {      
      19 +    if (rows.isEmpty()) {                                      
      20 +      return OptionalDouble.empty();                           
      21 +    }                                                          
      22 +    double model = rows.stream().mapToLong(r -> Math.abs(r.mode
         +lError())).sum();                                              
      23 +    double baseline = rows.stream().mapToLong(r -> Math.abs(r.b
         +aselineError())).sum();                                        
      24 +    if (baseline == 0) {                                       
      25 +      return OptionalDouble.empty();                           
      26 +    }                                                          
      27 +    return OptionalDouble.of(1 - model / baseline);            
      28 +  }                                                            
      29 +}
